· Journal of Language and Social Psychology

Janet Bavelas

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Janet-Bavelas

Nicole Chovil

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Nicole-Chovil

The authors propose that dialogue in face-to-face interaction is both audible and visible; language use in this setting includes visible acts of meaning such as facial displays and hand gestures. Several criteria distinguish these from other nonverbal acts: (a) They are sensitive to a sender-receiver relationship in that they are less likely to occur when an addressee will not see them, (b) they

... \[Show full abstract\]

https://www.researchgate.net/

are analogically encoded symbols, (c) their meaning can be explicated or demonstrated in context, and (d) they are fully integrated with the accompanying words, although they may be redundant or nonredundant with these words. For these particular acts, the authors eschew the term nonverbal communication because it is a negative definition based solely on physical source. Instead, they propose an integrated message model in which the moment-by-moment audible and visible communicative acts are treated as a unified whole.

October 2018

Janet Bavelas

Nicole Chovil

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Nicole-Chovil

Conversational facial gestures are not emotional expressions (Ekman, 1997). Facial gestures are co-speech gestures – configurations of the face, eyes, and/or head that are synchronized with words and other co-speech gestures. Facial gestures are the most frequent facial actions in dialogue, and the majority serve pragmatic (meta-communicative) rather than referential functions. A qualitative

... \[Show full abstract\]

https://www.researchgate.net/

microanalysis of a close-call story illustrates three pragmatic facial gestures in their macro- and micro-context: (a) The narrator's thinking faces (Goodwin & Goodwin, 1986) occurred as the narrator was getting started, and they accompanied verbal collateral signals of delay, such as “uh” or “um”. (b) The narrator pointed at his hand gestures with his head and eyes (Streeck, 1993), drawing the addressee's attention to depictions that would later be crucial to the close call. (c) The meta-communicative functions of smiles included marking the narrator's description of danger as ironic or humorous, hinting at key elements, and acknowledging errors.

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/241727096\_Interactive\_gestures#references

Illustrators are hand gestures made during conversation. Following Bavelas, Hagen, Lane, and Lawrie (1989), we propose a new division of illustrators, into topic and interactive gestures. Interactive gestures refer to the interlocutor rather than to the topic of conversation, and they help maintain the conversation as a social system. They subsume but are not limited to the category previously called beats or batons. Three tests of this theory are reported here. In Experiment 1, the same narrative task was assigned to both dyads and individuals: Dyads had a higher rate of interactive gestures than did individuals, but the opposite pattern was shown for topic gestures. In Experiment 2, we manipulated visual availability: The rate of interactive gestures was higher for partners interacting face‐to‐face than for those who could not see each other, but topic gestures were not significantly affected by condition. Thus, in both experiments, interactive and topic gestures responded differently to social variables, which strongly suggests they are functionally distinct groups. A final analysis showed that, in both data sets, interactive gestures were less redundant with the words they accompanied than were topic gestures, which supports our hypothesis that they maintain involvement with the interlocutor without interrupting the verbal flow of discourse.

Cristina-Ioana Galusca Cristina-Ioana Galusca

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Cristina-Ioana-Galusca-2

Gestures play a crucial role in human communication and cultural transmission, supporting both cognitive processing and listener comprehension. Because communicative contexts and listeners vary widely, gestures must flexibly adapt to effectively support communication. This paper explores how adults from different cultures adapt their gestural behavior according to listener presence, age, and speaker culture, three crucial factors that remain underexplored, particularly in settings with limited or absent real-time feedback (e.g., recordings). Here we examined French and German native speakers explaining novel words (i.e., a canonical example of cultural transmission) to present or imagined listeners, who were either adults or children. We analyzed gesture frequency, type (referential or non-referential), and salience (size or handedness (unimanual vs. bimanual)). Listener presence did not significantly influence any aspect of gestural behavior, whereas participants used more referential gestures when addressing children than adults. Additionally, French speakers produced more gestures overall, especially non-referential and bimanual gestures, than German speakers. Adults thus exhibit highly selective gesture adaptations in both cultures: referential gestures are selectively adjusted to listener age, while overall gesture rate remains stable across listener presence conditions, and cultural differences emerge primarily in non-referential and bimanual gestures. These patterns support a function-specific view of audience design in gesture production. This nuanced understanding informs theories of gesture production and has practical implications for enhancing communication in digital and educational contexts.

A Vision Based Framework Integrating Attention and Action Cues for Interpretable Cognitive Workload Assessment in Human Robot Collaborative Assembly

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/414315578\_A\_Vision\_Based\_Framework\_Integrating\_Attention\_and\_Action\_Cues\_for\_Interpretable\_Cognitive\_Workload\_Assessment\_in\_Human\_Robot\_Collaborative\_Assembly

Junyan Xionga

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Junyan-Xionga-2369277706

Daqiang Guo

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Daqiang-Guo-2356531342

The introduction of human-robot collaboration (HRC) in industrial assembly operations is revolutionizing the manufacturing landscape. In this evolving environment, operators are required to seamlessly coordinate their manual tasks with real-time task information and robotic behaviors. These demands fluctuate during operation, yet conventional workload assessments depend on body-worn physiological sensors that complicate practical deployment. Here, we present a vision-based attention--action framework for continuous and interpretable workload-related assessment in HRC assembly. The framework combines RGB-D observations with robot states and calibrated task-related areas to construct a temporally confirmed representation of operator behavior. This representation identifies where task demand is concentrated and explains how it develops when attention and action diverge, the task context changes, or the operator hesitates. We evaluated the framework in a three-level collaborative gearbox assembly experiment with ten participants, using subjective ratings and synchronized physiological signals as independent references. Raw NASA-TLX ratings confirmed increasing perceived workload across conditions, with significant effects on overall workload and its mental and temporal dimensions. The vision-derived HRC-CWL output was significantly associated with ECG-derived features in seven of nine participants with complete correlation data. Synchronized interaction episodes further showed temporal correspondence between detected hesitation and physiological activity. Real-time deployment demonstrated that the framework can operate without requiring operators to wear additional sensors. These findings support HRC-CWL as an interpretable behavioral proxy for cognitive ergonomics analysis and adaptive robot assistance, rather than a direct psychophysiological measure of workload.

Why teach English when you can perform it? Implementing drama in language teaching

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/408139273\_Why\_teach\_English\_when\_you\_can\_perform\_it\_Implementing\_drama\_in\_language\_teaching

Full-text available

Fatiha Belmerabet

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Fatiha-Belmerabet-2355512390

Drama is the oldest genre of human literary creation, characterized mainly by action and dialogue. When educators successfully integrate drama into educational settings, students gain opportunities to learn and practice English effectively. Though drama offers advantages, educators frequently encounter practical issues when implementing it in foreign language classrooms. This paper presents existing assumptions and research on the use of drama in language teaching. It also supplies educators with a thorough understanding of how drama supports foreign language learning. The paper first presents current theoretical frameworks supporting drama-based learning. Then, it identifies the role and benefits of drama in language education. It also addresses the main issues impeding implementation. In addition, the paper discusses practical strategies for using drama in the classroom and introduces useful drama activities. Finally, it provides practical recommendations to help teachers and learners effectively incorporate drama into English language learning. Keywords: Drama; Foreign Language Teaching; Foreign Language Learning; Implementation advantages and Challenges; Teaching Drama Methods

Investigating the role of pragmatic visual signals in language emergence using communication games and agent-based models

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/415527015\_Investigating\_the\_role\_of\_pragmatic\_visual\_signals\_in\_language\_emergence\_using\_communication\_games\_and\_agent-based\_models

COGN LINGUIST

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Lois-Dona-2371759553

Aslı Özyürek

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Asli-Oezyuerek-2371759240

Judith Holler

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Judith-Holler-2371743411

Limor Raviv Limor Raviv

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Limor-Raviv

Studying the emergence of language has often relied on communication games and agent-based models, with a strong focus on referential linguistic signals, often overlooking pragmatic signals that are frequently expressed visually, such as facial signals. These signals are a crucial component of natural interaction, supporting repair, coordination, and negotiation of meaning. We outline existing language emergence experiments, highlighting findings from communication games and research on pragmatic facial signals in interaction, demonstrating their relevance to the emergence of communication systems. Furthermore, computational models of language emergence, such as agent-based models, largely overlook that communication is layered across multiple communication channels. We argue that incorporating pragmatic visual signals into these approaches can help to provide a more nuanced understanding of the processes underlying language emergence in natural, multimodal interaction. Following these insights, we propose two complementary directions for future research. First, communication game paradigms can be adapted to systematically analyze visual pragmatic signals, linking them to existing commonly used measures such as convergence, communicative success, and adaptation of linguistic forms. Second, agent-based models can be extended to multi-channel frameworks in which agents produce and interpret both referential and pragmatic signals, enabling investigation of how layered multimodal information shapes language emergence.

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/415527015\_Investigating\_the\_role\_of\_pragmatic\_visual\_signals\_in\_language\_emergence\_using\_communication\_games\_and\_agent-based\_models

Establishment and application of gesture-pragmatic corpus of chinese kindergarten teachers (G-PCCKT)

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/408654920\_Establishment\_and\_application\_of\_gesture-pragmatic\_corpus\_of\_chinese\_kindergarten\_teachers\_G-PCCKT

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Zhe-Wang-2357153281

This study develops the Gesture-Pragmatic Corpus of Chinese Kindergarten Teachers (G-PCCKT) using ELAN to investigate teachers' gesture-pragmatic functions. The corpus construction involves transcribing speech, annotating multifunctional gestures through a custom Functional Annotation Scheme based on an Integrated Functional Categorization of Gesture , and coding three pragmatic aspects: demonstratives, illocutionary forces, and question-initiated sequences. As a reliable tool for data retrieval and analysis, the G-PCCKT enables multifaceted investigation into the pragmatic functions of gestures used by Chinese kindergarten teachers, including: (i) clarifying deictic references, (ii) reinforcing illocutionary force, and (iii) facilitating question formulation and sequence organization. Preliminary results indicate that: (i) teachers' gesture use varies with different types of demonstratives to aid children's comprehension; (ii) gestures previously regarded as “meaningless” reveal performative significance when examined in pragmatic context; and (iii) gestures support teachers in managing classroom pacing by smoothly transitioning between question-initiated sequences.

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/408654920\_Establishment\_and\_application\_of\_gesture-pragmatic\_corpus\_of\_chinese\_kindergarten\_teachers\_G-PCCKT

Gesture Studies

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/408385730\_Gesture\_Studies

Keiko Tsuchiya

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Keiko-Tsuchiya-2356306738

Research on manual gestures is one of the main streams in language and multimodality study, which takes cognitive scientific, psycholinguistic experimental, and Anthro-linguistic approaches. This chapter first reviews the development of the theoretical discussion on whether speech and gesture (hand gestures, in particular) share a common computational structure, providing a review on the concept of imagery. It is followed by a classification of manual gestures on the timeline from classic, modern, and the present time, for example, semantic and pragmatic gestures, recurrent gestures, and interactive gestures. The chapter then delineates some case studies on cross-cultural analysis of hand gestures and the effects of use of hand gestures in learning.

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/408385730\_Gesture\_Studies

Recurrent head movements in DGS and spoken German: Communicative functions of head nods and head shakes in dyadic interaction

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/405253484\_Recurrent\_head\_movements\_in\_DGS\_and\_spoken\_German\_Communicative\_functions\_of\_head\_nods\_and\_head\_shakes\_in\_dyadic\_interaction

Full-text available

Anastasia Bauer Anastasia Bauer

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Anastasia-Bauer

Silva Ladewig Silva Ladewig

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Silva-Ladewig

While recurrent manual gestures have been extensively studied, nonmanual actions such as head movements remain less explored, especially in naturalistic interaction. This study investigates whether head nods and head shakes serve communicative functions comparable to those of recurrent manual gestures. Drawing on corpus data from spoken German and DGS (German Sign Language), we analyze approximately 1 h of interaction per language with 16 participants. More than 2,500 head movements produced by speakers/signers and their addressees were manually identified and annotated. The findings reveal a largely shared functional repertoire of head nods and head shakes across spoken and signed interaction, with no qualitative differences in the range of functions. In both languages, head movements fulfill discursive, stance-taking, illocutionary, and interactive functions, as well as affirmation and negation, closely paralleling patterns described for recurrent manual gestures. At the same time, cross-linguistic differences emerge in the relative prominence and degree of grammaticalization of specific functions, most notably in the use of head shakes for negation in DGS and for stance-taking in spoken German. Extending a functional framework for recurrent manual gestures to nonmanual actions, this study shows that head nods and head shakes exhibit the defining properties of recurrency: stable form–function pairings that recur systematically across speakers, signers, and interactional context. These findings support an embodied view of language and highlight the role of head movements as key semiotic resources in spoken and signed interaction.

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/405253484\_Recurrent\_head\_movements\_in\_DGS\_and\_spoken\_German\_Communicative\_functions\_of\_head\_nods\_and\_head\_shakes\_in\_dyadic\_interaction

Gesture and coparticipation in the activity of searching for a word

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/265103342\_Gesture\_and\_coparticipation\_in\_the\_activity\_of\_searching\_for\_a\_word

Full-text available

https://www.researchgate.net/journal/Semiotica-1613-3692

Marjorie Goodwin Marjorie Goodwin

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Marjorie-Goodwin

Charles Goodwin Charles Goodwin

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Charles-Goodwin-2

In this paper gesture will be studied by analyzing in some detail its organization within a particular activity, searching for a word. Such an approach is quite different from others that often study such phenomena by isolating gesture from the local, interactive circumstances of its production (see, for example, Morris et al. 1979). However, by investigat-ing gesture within particular events, it is possible to begin to study in some detail not only how participants find it to be meaningful, but also how they use that meaningfulness as a constitutive feature of the social organization of the activities they are engaged in. Data for this analysis consist of videotapes of conversations recorded in a range of natural settings (for a more complete description of these data see C. Goodwin \[1981: 33-46\]). We will begin by raising the issue of how participants find gesture to be a meaningful event. In the following, a speaker produces a small gesture, a wave of her hand, and immediately after this happens, the recipient nods toward her. Thus two parties are clearly working in concert; an action is performed by one and answered by another. However, how these participants interpret each other's actions, and even what they are doing together, remains inaccessible unless the activity they are involved in, and the types of coparticipation that activity makes possible, are investigated in detail. Talk is transcribed using a simplified version of the Jefferson transcription system (Sacks et al. 1974: 731-733). Dashes within paren-theses mark tenths of seconds with a silence; a full second is marked by a plus sign (Example I). 1 Example L (G. 126.788) hand gesture I A: We have the top bunks y'know in the urn, (+) ΐ Β: nods Semiotica 62-1/2 (1986), 51-75. By virtue of its sequential placement right after the gesture, the nod appears to constitute a response to the gesture. If this is correct, the recipient has in some particular way understood the gesture and re-sponded to it. This raises the question of what the recipient understood the gesture to be. Unlike some gestures, such as those examined by Morris et al. (1979), this is not a Stereotypie hand movement that encodes a clearly recognized meaning. Moreover, unlike many hand movements which obtain a clearly visible sense because of the talk which accompanies them, this hand gesture occurs in the midst of a silence. How then, was the recipient able to understand this gesture? In this paper we will argue that such a gesture obtains its meaning by virtue of its placement within an activity which is clearly recognizable to the participants — a word search. In order to show this, we will examine the interactive structure of word searches in some detail. We discover first, that as activities, word searches provide organization for a wide range of vocal and nonvocal phenomena, including both Stereotypie and nonstere-otypic gestures, and second, that participants attend to such phenomena because they are part of the currency through which appropriate coparti-cipation in the activity is displayed and negotiated. Evidence that participants recognize an activity that can be loosely glossed as searching for a word, is provided by the following. After A pauses in mid-sentence, B (who also attended the wedding A is describing) provides a .word that could complete that sentence (Example 2).

Two preferences in the organization of reference to persons in conversation and their interaction

https://www.researchgate.net/publication/248179807\_Two\_preferences\_in\_the\_organization\_of\_reference\_to\_persons\_in\_conversation\_and\_their\_interaction

Full-text available

Harvey Sacks

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Harvey-Sacks-82236999

Emanuel A. Schegloff Emanuel A. Schegloff

https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Emanuel-Schegloff

Introduction Research into the social organization of conversation has, as one type of product, the isolation of a 'preference' operating for some domain(s) of conversation and the depiction of the organizational machinery through which that preference is effected. A variety of such preferences and their organizational instruments have been studied. It regularly turns out that several of them are concurrently relevant, concurrently applicable and concurrently satisfied. However, on some occasions in which some such two preferences appear to figure, the actually produced talk does not concurrently satisfy them. Examination of such materials is particularly useful. They can, for example, give support to the proposal that separate preferences are involved, a possibility that the regularity of their concurrent satisfaction obscures. Furthermore, examination of such materials permits the extraction of a 'second-order' organization directed to an integration of preferences on occasions when their potential concurrent satisfiability is not realized. The study of various such second-order devices suggests that they do provide resources that organize adjustment of the concurrently applicable preferences when both are not satisfiable. What is more interesting is that the second-order devices themselves represent types of solutions, a common one being to prefer satisfaction of one of the applicable preferences, and the other being relaxed to such a point as will allow the preferred to be achieved: The non-preferred of the two is not suspended but 'relaxed step by step'.

Social Class, Linguistic Codes and Grammatical Elements

LANG SPEECH

Basil Bernstein

https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Basil-Bernstein-2077648601

In a previous paper (Bernstein, 1962) two general types of linguistic code, elaborated and restricted, were proposed. These codes were regarded as functions of different forms of social relationships. The codes were thought to entail qualitatively different verbal planning orientations which control different modes of self-regulation and levels of cognitive behaviour. Social class differences in the use of these codes were expected. Speech samples were obtained and the hesitation phenomena analysed, from a discussion situation involving small groups of middle and working class subjects with varying I.Q. profiles. It was found that the middle-class groups used a shorter phrase length and a longer pause interval than the working-class group. These differences in the hesitation phenomena were sharper when working-class and middle-class groups, matched for intelligence on a group verbal and non-verbal test, were compared. It was considered that the members of the two class groups were oriented to qualitatively different levels of verbal planning which control lexicon and structural selections. The working-class groups were thought to be making selections from a lower level of the linguistic hierarchy; whilst the middle-class subjects irrespective of verbal I.Q. were oriented to making selections from a higher level of the hierarchy. This paper will report the analysis of the speech.

During a narrative discourse, accessibility of the referents is rarely fixed once and for all. Rather, each referent varies in accessibility as the discourse unfolds, depending on the presence and prominence of the other referents. This leads the speaker to use various referential expressions to refer to the main protagonists of the story at different moments in the narrative. This study relies

... \[Show full abstract\]

https://www.researchgate.net/

on a new, collaborative storytelling in sequence task designed to assess how speakers adjust their referential choices when they refer to different characters at specific discourse stages corresponding to the introduction, maintaining, or shift of the character in focus, in increasingly complex referential contexts. Referential complexity of the stories was manipulated through variations in the number of characters (1 vs. 2) and, for stories in which there were two characters, in their ambiguity in gender (different vs. same gender). Data were coded for the type of reference markers as well as the type of reference content (i.e., the extent of the information provided in the referential expression). Results showed that, beyond the expected effects of discourse stages on reference markers (more indefinite markers at the introduction stage, more pronouns at the maintaining stage, and more definite markers at the shift stage), the number of characters and their ambiguity in gender also modulated speakers' referential choices at specific discourse stages, For the maintaining stage, an effect of the number of characters was observed for the use of pronouns and of definite markers, with more pronouns when there was a single character, sometimes replaced by definite expressions when two characters were present in the story. For the shift stage, an effect of gender ambiguity was specifically noted for the reference content with more specific information provided in the referential expression when there was referential ambiguity. Reference content is an aspect of referential marking that is rarely addressed in a narrative context, yet it revealed a quite flexible referential behavior by the speakers.

The need to visualize large social networks is growing as hardware capabilities make analyzing large networks feasible and many new data sets become available. Unfortunately, the visualizations in existing systems do not satisfactorily answer the basic dilemma of being readable both for the global structure of the network and also for detailed analysis of local communities. To address this

... \[Show full abstract\]

https://www.researchgate.net/

problem, we present NodeTrix, a hybrid representation for networks that combines the advantages of two traditional representations: node-link diagrams are used to show the global structure of a network, while arbitrary portions of the network can be shown as adjacency matrices to better support the analysis of communities. A key contribution is a set of interaction techniques. These allow analysts to create a NodeTrix visualization by dragging selections from either a node-link or a matrix, flexibly manipulate the NodeTrix representation to explore the dataset, and create meaningful summary visualizations of their findings. Finally, we present a case study applying NodeTrix to the analysis of the InfoVis 2004 coauthorship dataset to illustrate the capabilities of NodeTrix as both an exploration tool and an effective means of communicating results.

# A Primazia da Metacomunicação Relacional e da Preservação da Face no Intercâmbio Humano Continuado

Nas interações humanas contínuas e de alta densidade afetiva ou hierárquica, a transmissão formal de informação proposicional figura como uma camada superficial da dinâmica comunicativa. A premissa analítica fundamental da pragmática interacional estabelece que todo intercâmbio de conteúdo (o nível digital ou informativo) encontra-se estritamente subordinado à metacomunicação de poder, controle e posicionamento relacional (o nível analógico ou contextual) \[cite: 1, 2\]. Nesse ecossistema de trocas interpessoais, a preservação da imagem social e a manutenção da estabilidade do sistema relacional antecedem e frequentemente anulam as exigências da lógica formal, do rigor factual ou da consistência argumentativa \[cite: 3, 4\].

A arquitetura dessa subordinação manifesta-se pela sobreposição de múltiplos vetores pragmáticos, que englobam os axiomas da comunicação interpessoal, a gestão tática de ameaças à imagem, as implicaturas conversacionais invisíveis, o recurso à ambiguidade intencional sob cenários de conflito e a paralisia estrutural induzida por injunções paradoxais.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A Arquitetura Metacomunicativa: Níveis de Conteúdo, Analógico vs. Digital e Pontuação

A base da pragmática humana repousa sobre os princípios formulados pela Escola de Palo Alto. O pressuposto axiomático primário postula a impossibilidade absoluta da não comunicação: em qualquer contexto relacional, todo comportamento — seja o silêncio ostensivo, a esquiva visual ou a imobilidade — possui valor de mensagem e força de atuação interpessoal \[cite: 2\]. Nenhuma pessoa em um ecossistema interpessoal consegue abster-se de emitir sinais sobre como percebe a si mesma e ao outro.

O segundo pilar axiomático estabelece a cisão estrutural entre o nível de conteúdo e o nível de relação \[cite: 1, 2\]. O nível de conteúdo refere-se aos dados brutos transmitidos digitalmente por meio da linguagem verbal formal e das suas propriedades denotativas. O nível de relação, expressado primordialmente por canais analógicos — tais como prosódia, microexpressões faciais, postura corporal, distância interpessoal e timing —, atua como uma metacomunicação \[cite: 1, 2, 5\]. Esta metacomunicação funciona como uma instrução de decodificação que determina como o conteúdo verbal deve ser interpretado, operando simultaneamente como um mapeamento de poder que define os papéis de dominância, submissão, proximidade ou distância \[cite: 1, 6\].

Dimensão Pragmática

Comunicação Digital (Conteúdo)

Comunicação Analógica (Relação)

Meio de Expressão

Símbolos linguísticos arbitrários, sintaxe verbal e lógica proposicional.

Expressões faciais, gestos, prosódia, postura e contextos espaciais \[cite: 5, 7\].

Função Primária

Transmissão de informações factuais e significados denotativos.

Definição da natureza da relação, posições de poder e afeto \[cite: 1, 2\].

Capacidade Lógica

Alta precisão para abstração, negação lógica e temporalidade.

Baixa capacidade para expressar operadores lógicos complexos ou negações explícitas \[cite: 2\].

Grau de Vulnerabilidade

Sujeito ao escrutínio da verdade factual e verificação lógica.

Ambíguo, de difícil refutação e ideal para a preservação tática da imagem \[cite: 3, 8\].

A organização do fluxo interativo depende substancialmente da pontuação da sequência de fatos \[cite: 2\]. Os participantes de uma relação continuada percebem a cadeia de eventos como uma sequência causal, mas habitualmente arbitram o ponto de origem dessa sequência de forma unilateral para justificar seus próprios comportamentos defensivos ou agressivos. Essa discrepância na pontuação gera um padrão em que cada ator considera a sua própria atitude como uma mera reação necessária ao comportamento do outro, perpetuando conflitos nos quais a verdade factual é irrelevante frente ao cabo de guerra pelo controle da narrativa relacional.

As relações estabilizam-se sob duas formas relacionais distintas: a simetria e a complementariedade \[cite: 6\]. A relação simétrica fundamenta-se na igualdade e no espelhamento do comportamento do interlocutor; quando o desejo de preservação da posição de poder é ameaçado, a simetria pode degenerar em uma escalada competitiva de retaliações no nível analógico. Por sua vez, a relação complementar baseia-se na máxima diferenciação das posições, onde um interlocutor assume a posição superior e o outro a posição inferior \[cite: 6\]. A rigidez patológica em qualquer uma dessas formas reflete a tentativa contínua de defender a imagem e a estabilidade das posições de poder, subordinando a lógica formal das conversas à manutenção da estrutura relacional acordada ou disputada.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A Microdinâmica da Preservação da Face e Insinuações Invisíveis: Goffman, Brown & Levinson e Grice

A defesa da imagem social é a força reguladora que impede a comunicação puramente direta e informativa na maioria das interações interpessoais. O conceito de face compreende a imagem pública e o valor interpessoal que um indivíduo reivindica para si mesmo durante um contato social. Brown e Levinson, expandindo a obra conceitual de Erving Goffman, desdobram a imagem social em dois vetores fundamentais: a face positiva, definida pelo desejo intrínseco de ser aprovado, estimado, validado e reconhecido pelos pares; e a face negativa, que representa o anseio premente pela não imposição externa, caracterizado pela reivindicação de autonomia, liberdade de ação e proteção contra intromissões indesejadas \[cite: 4\].

Durante as trocas comunicativas cotidianas, incontáveis enunciados configuram Atos Ameaçadores à Imagem (

Face-Threatening Acts

). Um pedido direto ou uma ordem explícita violam a face negativa do receptor, ao passo que críticas, desacordos ou demonstrações de desaprovação atingem diretamente a sua face positiva \[cite: 4\]. Para mitigar essas ameaças e garantir a coexistência dentro do sistema relacional, os atores lançam mão de estratégias de polidez pragmática que modificam o enquadramento proposicional das mensagens.

É no espaço entre a polidez necessária e o Princípio de Cooperação de Paul Grice que surge a comunicação indireta e a insinuação invisível. Grice postula que a conversação humana é norteada por uma expectativa implícita de cooperação, operacionalizada por quatro máximas pragmáticas fundamentais. A máxima de quantidade estabelece que o emissor deve fornecer exatamente a carga informativa exigida pela interação, sem omissões nem excessos. A máxima de qualidade proíbe a afirmação de enunciados reconhecidamente falsos ou desprovidos de evidências adequadas. A máxima de relação impõe que as contribuições sejam estritamente pertinentes ao foco temático do diálogo. Por fim, a máxima de modo exige clareza, objetividade e a eliminação de ambiguidades ou prolixidades deliberadas.

Quando um interlocutor viabiliza a violação ostensiva e intencional de uma dessas máximas sem abandonar formalmente o fluxo interativo, ele gera uma implicatura conversacional. A mensagem manifesta no código digital passa a abrigar um significado implícito profundamente divergente do seu sentido literal. Ao violar a máxima de relação ao responder a uma cobrança sobre responsabilidade com uma observação desconexa sobre o tom de voz do questionador, o emissor recusa-se a submeter-se ao enquadramento do interlocutor sem articular uma recusa formal \[cite: 9\]. Essa manobra transfere integralmente a carga de decodificação da verdade ao ouvinte. Se o interlocutor reagir à insinuação oculta, o emissor pode acionar uma denegação plausível, alegando ter dito apenas o texto literal. Dessa forma, a manipulação das máximas de Grice serve como um escudo protetor para a face do emissor e uma arma metacomunicativa silenciosa para testar e demarcar o equilíbrio de poder.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Ambiguidade Tática, Conflito de Dupla Esquiva e Multimodalidade: A Teoria da Equivocação de Bavelas

Quando as contingências sociais impõem exigências contraditórias nas quais qualquer resposta direta causaria prejuízos catastróficos à imagem dos interlocutores, o sistema comunicativo recorre à ambiguidade tática. A Teoria da Equivocação, desenvolvida por Janet Beavin Bavelas e colaboradores, demonstra que mensagens evasivas, vagas, oblíquas ou contraditórias não constituem um defeito individual de formulação nem uma falha moral, mas representam a resposta sistemática e previsível a um Conflito de Dupla Esquiva (

Avoidance-Avoidance Conflict

) \[cite: 3, 8, 10\].

O mecanismo gerador do Conflito de Dupla Esquiva ocorre quando o emissor é confrontado com uma situação na qual responder com a verdade nua e crua implicaria destruir a face do interlocutor ou expor a própria vulnerabilidade relacional (Primeira Opção Indesejada), enquanto mentir abertamente violaria valores morais, padrões institucionais ou resultaria em severa retaliação caso a falsidade seja desmascarada (Segunda Opção Indesejada) \[cite: 10\]. Diante de um impasse no qual ambas as alternativas de resposta direta conduzem a consequências profundamente insatisfatórias, o ator esquiva-se do dilema produzindo uma mensagem propositalmente ambígua \[cite: 3, 10\]. A equivocação funciona, portanto, como uma solução adaptativa para navegar em cenários de alta tensão social \[cite: 3\].

A avaliação do nível de equivocação contido em uma mensagem é operacionalizada por Bavelas mediante um modelo de quatro dimensões pragmáticas independentes, conforme estruturado a seguir \[cite: 3, 9, 11\].

Dimensão da Equivocação

Descrição Pragmática

Sinalizador de Ambiguidade Tática

O grau de clareza e precisão com que o tema principal é abordado na mensagem \[cite: 3, 11\].

Ocorre o uso de frases incompletas, abstrações excessivas, jargões obscuros ou shifting de tópicos \[cite: 3, 9\].

A extensão em que a mensagem reflete a opinião ou o compromisso do próprio falante \[cite: 3, 9, 11\].

Emprego de voz passiva, pronomes indefinidos ("dizem que", "alguém sugeriu") ou negação de autoria proposicional \[cite: 11\].

A medida em que a fala é diretamente endereçada ao interlocutor específico presente na interação \[cite: 3, 9, 11\].

Modificação do escopo de alocução, respondendo como se falasse a um público geral ou a um terceiro ausente \[cite: 9\].

O nível de conexão direta e pertinência da resposta em relação à pergunta ou estímulo imediatamente anterior \[cite: 3, 9, 11\].

Tangencialidade extrema, respostas enquadradas como contra-perguntas ou desvio da agenda conversacional \[cite: 9\].

No plano da comunicação interpessoal face a face, a ambiguidade tática estende-se para além do canal estritamente linguístico, valendo-se da multimodalidade integradora \[cite: 1, 12\]. A perspectiva microanalítica desenvolvida por Bavelas refuta o modelo tradicional e simplista de emissor-receptor herdado da engenharia de telecomunicações do século XX, demonstrando que o diálogo é um processo co-construído de ação conjunta em tempo real \[cite: 12, 13\].

Em vez de ser uma simples transmissão encadeada de pacotes de dados, a comunicação interpessoal avança mediante sequências contínuas e simultâneas de microajustes e calibrações interpessoais \[cite: 12\]. Nesse ambiente, os gestos co-verbais (

co-speech gestures

) e as expressões faciais especializadas atuam como elementos constitutivos da proposição, e não como meras ilustrações redundantes \[cite: 1, 7, 14\].

Os gestos dividem-se em gestos tópicos, que retratam o conteúdo factual sob discussão, e gestos interativos, que desempenham um papel estritamente relacional \[cite: 13, 15\]. Os gestos interativos não transmitem dados sobre o tema da conversa, mas referem-se diretamente ao interlocutor e à manutenção do canal social \[cite: 13, 15\]. Eles coordenam a alternância de turnos, solicitam confirmação implícita de entendimento ou modulam a intensidade das afirmações informadas \[cite: 13, 15, 16\].

Através da combinação multimodal entre verbalização fluida, gestos interativos sutis e hesitações prosódicas controladas, o emissor consegue introduzir ideias no ambiente interativo em caráter experimental \[cite: 3, 7, 15\]. O falante testa a receptividade do outro ao posicionamento de poder sem assumir um compromisso proposicional estrito. Se o receptor demonstrar resistência analógica, o emissor pode recuar sem perdas substanciais de face, alegando que o gesto ou a ambiguidade expressa não continham a carga assertiva percebida.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A Estrutura Patológica do Duplo Vínculo: Paradoxos e Paralisia Interacional

A manifestação mais extrema da subordinação da lógica à metacomunicação ocorre no fenômeno do Duplo Vínculo (

Double Bind

), formulado por Gregory Bateson e colaboradores no âmbito das pesquisas sobre esquizofrenia e comunicação sistêmica \[cite: 6, 17\]. O duplo vínculo é uma estrutura de interação paradoxal recorrente que imobiliza o receptor do ponto de vista lógico e comportamental, eliminando qualquer via de fuga racional ou metacomunicativa \[cite: 6\].

A consolidação de um duplo vínculo exige a presença contínua de cinco elementos encadeados. O primeiro ingrediente é uma relação de alta intensidade e dependência, na qual a sobrevivência física, emocional, institucional ou existencial da vítima depende criticamente do vínculo com o outro ator \[cite: 6\]. O segundo elemento é a experiência reiterada, garantindo que o padrão paradoxal não seja um evento isolado, mas uma constante previsível que molda a estrutura cognitiva do indivíduo \[cite: 6\].

O terceiro componente é a injunção negativa primária, transmitida habitualmente por via digital e verbal, articulada sob a forma de uma ameaça expressa: a ordem exige que a pessoa cumpra determinada conduta sob pena de punição severa ou retirada do afeto \[cite: 6\]. O quarto elemento é a injunção negativa secundária, emitida em um nível metacomunicativo mais abstrato (geralmente por meio de pistas analógicas, tom de voz ou postura corporal) \[cite: 6\]. Esta segunda ordem entra em contradição direta com a primeira, proibindo o cumprimento do comando primário sob ameaça de igual punição \[cite: 6\].

Por fim, o quinto elemento é a injunção negativa terciária, uma norma sistêmica implícita que proíbe terminantemente a vítima de abandonar o campo interpessoal e, simultaneamente, pune qualquer tentativa de metacomunicar sobre o paradoxo \[cite: 6\]. A pessoa é impedida de apontar a incompatibilidade entre as duas ordens, ficando encarcerada no circuito.

A paralisia do receptor em um duplo vínculo decorre do colapso da distinção entre os níveis de linguagem descritos por Bertrand Russell na Teoria dos Tipos Lógicos. Ao receber duas ordens mutuamente exclusivas em níveis metacomunicativos opostos, qualquer ação que o indivíduo adote violará uma das injunções \[cite: 6\]. Se ele cumpre o comando digital, ataca a exigência analógica; se submete-se ao sinal analógico, desobedece à instrução digital. Diferente de um dilema simples no qual se pode escolher o menor dos males, no duplo vínculo o direito de escolha é subtraído, e a tentativa de denunciar o absurdo do paradoxo é rotulada como insolência, loucura ou agressão relacional. A preservação do poder do emissor sobrepuja a lógica proposicional, trancando o receptor numa cela pragmática.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Síntese Interacional e Implicações Sistêmicas

A integração das teorias da metacomunicação, preservação da face, implicaturas Griceanas, equivocação tática e paradoxos estruturais demonstra uma verdade fundamental sobre a vida relacional continuada: a razão formal e a precisão dos fatos são meras ferramentas contextuais, e não os fins da comunicação humana.

Quando ocorrem intercâmbios interpessoais, os atores estão envolvidos primariamente na gestão do seu

de poder e no monitoramento constante das suas posições de vulnerabilidade. A tabela a seguir sintetiza a subordinação do conteúdo digital aos imperativos da metacomunicação e da preservação da face em diferentes cenários interpessoais.

Cenário Interativo

Estratégia Pragmática Empregada

Mecanismo Metacomunicativo Subjacente

Efeito no Nível do Conteúdo (Lógica)

Conflito de Posição Simétrica

Escalada de acusações e inversão arbitrária da pontuação causal \[cite: 2\].

Disputa aberta pelo controle do topo da hierarquia interpessoal.

Negligência total das evidências factuais; o foco reside em ter a palavra final.

Ameaça Dupla à Face (AAC)

\[cite: 3, 10\]

Equivocação e ambiguidade tática multimodal \[cite: 3, 12\].

Preservação da imagem pública e denegação plausível de compromissos \[cite: 4\].

Fracionamento sintático, opacidade proposicional e deslocamento de contexto \[cite: 3, 11\].

Provocação Oculta

Quebra ostensiva de Máxima Griceana gerando implicatura.

Teste de autoridade sem exposição de vulnerabilidade explícita.

Aparente irrelevância textual que obriga o receptor a decodificar a ameaça subentendida.

Retenção do Controle Sistêmico

Imposição de Duplo Vínculo estruturado \[cite: 6, 17\].

Paralisia pragmática do subjugado e interdição do questionamento \[cite: 6\].

Invalidação completa dos operadores lógicos universais e impossibilidade de resposta correta \[cite: 6\].

Em última análise, os dados teóricos e empíricos acumulados pelas ciências da linguagem e do comportamento confirmam que a comunicação interpessoal contínua opera como um ecossistema de regulação mútua. As palavras assumem o papel de veículos táticos mobilizados para garantir o equilíbrio do sistema relacional, resguardar a integridade psicossocial dos atores através da preservação da face e definir quem possui a autoridade de estipular o sentido do contexto. Sempre que a lógica estrita entra em rota de colisão com a necessidade de proteção da imagem ou com a manutenção das posições de poder relacional, a lógica é inevitavelmente sacrificada em favor da sobrevivência do posicionamento sistêmico dos interlocutores.
